Fair ASR: Re-Evaluating Black-Box Jailbreaks under Shared Target-Call Budgets
Authors: Zhida He, Xiaoyu Wen, Han Qi, Ziyuan Zhou, Peng Yu, Jiajia Li, Chaochao Lu, Qiaosheng Zhang
First: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00
Comments: 29 pages, 8 figures, 13 tables. Code: https://github.com/xsddys/Fair-ASR
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Reliable jailbreak evaluation is essential for assessing LLM safety, but most existing studies rely solely on attack success rate (ASR) without accounting for its dependence on attack budgets, resulting in unfair comparisons across methods. Existing compute-aware evaluations reduce heterogeneous resources into FLOPs, which is difficult to estimate for black-box models and fails to capture resource-specific constraints. To provide a comparable evaluation basis, we introduce Fair-ASR, an evaluation protocol for black-box jailbreak attacks under shared target-call budgets B, using target calls as a directly observable and method-agnostic comparison axis while tracking attacker calls separately for efficiency analysis. We re-evaluate 11 representative attacks under the Fair-ASR protocol and find that attack rankings change substantially across target-call budgets, simple stochastic perturbations and hand-crafted templates remain highly competitive under equal target access, and no evaluated LLM-driven method is efficient in both target and attacker calls. Motivated by this efficiency gap, we introduce ReCode, a compositional budget-efficient attack that combines desensitization rewriting with two effective low-cost primitives identified by Fair-ASR. Under a budget of 20 target calls, ReCode achieves 85% ASR on GPT-5 while requiring only 7.19 attacker calls per request on average, showing strong efficiency in both target and attacker calls.
中文标题/摘要
标题：公平ASR：在共享目标调用预算下重新评估黑箱越狱
可靠的越狱评估对于评估大型语言模型（LLM）的安全性至关重要，但现有大多数研究仅依赖攻击成功率（ASR），而未考虑其对攻击预算的依赖，导致方法之间的不公平比较。现有的计算感知评估将异构资源简化为FLOPs，这对于黑箱模型难以估计，并且未能捕捉特定资源的约束。为了提供可比较的评估基础，我们引入了公平ASR，这是一种在共享目标调用预算B下评估黑箱越狱攻击的评估协议，使用目标调用作为直接可观察和方法无关的比较轴，同时单独跟踪攻击者调用以进行效率分析。我们在公平ASR协议下重新评估了11个代表性攻击，发现攻击排名在目标调用预算之间发生了显著变化，简单的随机扰动和手工制作的模板在平等目标访问下仍然具有高度竞争力，并且没有评估的LLM驱动方法在目标和攻击者调用中都有效率。受到这一效率差距的启发，我们引入了ReCode，这是一种组合预算高效的攻击，结合了去敏感重写和公平ASR识别的两种有效低成本原语。在20个目标调用的预算下，ReCode在GPT-5上实现了85%的ASR，同时平均每个请求仅需7.19个攻击者调用，显示出在目标和攻击者调用中的强效率。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the need for reliable evaluations of black-box jailbreak attacks on large language models (LLMs), highlighting that existing studies often rely solely on attack success rates (ASR) without considering the impact of attack budgets, leading to unfair comparisons. Previous methods have struggled with estimating compute resources for black-box models and fail to account for resource-specific constraints. The proposed Fair-ASR evaluation protocol offers a more equitable comparison by using shared target-call budgets and tracking attacker calls separately, which reveals significant shifts in attack rankings based on budget variations. The paper contributes by introducing ReCode, a budget-efficient attack method that combines desensitization rewriting with low-cost primitives, achieving an 85% ASR on GPT-5 with only 7.19 attacker calls per request under a 20 target call budget, demonstrating improved efficiency in both target and attacker calls. This methodology provides a more accurate assessment of attack effectiveness and efficiency in the context of resource constraints.
本研究关注于评估大型语言模型（LLM）安全性所需的可靠越狱评估，强调现有研究主要关注攻击成功率（ASR），而未考虑攻击预算的影响，导致不公平比较。以往的方法在估算黑箱模型的计算资源方面存在困难，且未能有效捕捉特定资源约束。提出的Fair-ASR协议通过在共享目标调用预算下评估黑箱越狱攻击，提供了一种解决方案，使得不同方法之间的比较更加公平，同时单独分析攻击者调用以提高效率。本文的贡献在于使用Fair-ASR协议重新评估11种代表性攻击，揭示了攻击排名在目标调用预算下的显著变化，并表明传统方法在目标和攻击者调用方面都不够高效。该方法论包括引入ReCode，这是一种预算高效的攻击，结合了去敏感重写和低成本原语，在20个目标调用的预算下，ReCode在GPT-5上实现了85%的ASR，平均每个请求仅需7.19个攻击者调用，从而支持提高越狱评估效率的目标。
Making Your LLMs More Objective: Stabilizing LLM Safety Behavior Across Traits with Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning
Authors: Lang Cao
First: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Aligned large language models (LLMs) are expected to exhibit safety behavior based on the content of the user request: they should refuse unsafe requests and comply with safe ones. However, we show that the same request can elicit substantially different safety decisions under different traits assigned in the system prompt, a failure mode we call trait-induced safety variation. To measure this failure, we introduce refusal-based metrics: Trait-Induced Deviation measures dataset-level deviation from the no-trait baseline, while Trait-Induced Flip Rate measures whether the same request receives different safety decisions across traits. We then provide a representation-level analysis of the mechanism behind trait-induced safety shifts and find that traits perturb the model's safety representations within a low-dimensional subspace. To achieve trait-invariant safety, where safety behavior remains stable across traits, we introduce Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), a simple yet effective self-distillation framework that aligns an LLM's trait-conditioned behavior with its no-trait behavior. Guided by our analysis, we further propose Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN), an instantiation of TIST, which enforces invariance only within the identified trait subspace. Experiments show that TraSN improves trait-invariant safety and strengthens harmful-request safety while preserving general capability. Our results highlight traits as an important factor in LLM safety and robust model behavior.
中文标题/摘要
标题：使您的大型语言模型更客观：通过特征不变安全调优稳定大型语言模型的安全行为
对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）预计会根据用户请求的内容表现出安全行为：它们应该拒绝不安全的请求并遵守安全的请求。然而，我们展示了同一请求在系统提示中分配的不同特征下可能引发显著不同的安全决策，这种失败模式我们称之为特征引起的安全变异。为了衡量这种失败，我们引入了基于拒绝的指标：特征引起的偏差衡量数据集级别的偏差与无特征基线的偏差，而特征引起的翻转率衡量同一请求在不同特征下是否收到不同的安全决策。然后，我们提供了特征引起的安全变化机制的表示级分析，发现特征在低维子空间内扰动模型的安全表示。为了实现特征不变安全，即安全行为在不同特征间保持稳定，我们引入了特征不变安全调优（TIST），这是一种简单而有效的自蒸馏框架，将LLM的特征条件行为与其无特征行为对齐。在我们的分析指导下，我们进一步提出了特征子空间中和（TraSN），这是TIST的一种实例，强制在识别的特征子空间内保持不变。实验表明，TraSN提高了特征不变安全性，并增强了对有害请求的安全性，同时保持了整体能力。我们的结果强调特征是LLM安全性和稳健模型行为的重要因素。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the issue of safety behavior in aligned large language models (LLMs), which can vary significantly based on the traits assigned in system prompts, leading to inconsistent safety decisions for the same request. Previous methods did not adequately account for this trait-induced safety variation, resulting in unreliable model behavior. The proposed approach, Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), aims to stabilize safety behavior across different traits by aligning the model's trait-conditioned responses with its baseline no-trait behavior. This is achieved through a self-distillation framework, with an additional method called Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN) that enforces invariance within a specific trait subspace. Experimental results demonstrate that TraSN enhances trait-invariant safety and improves the model's ability to handle harmful requests while maintaining overall performance, thus supporting the goal of consistent safety behavior in LLMs.
本研究解决了对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）在安全行为方面的不一致性问题，这种不一致性会导致根据系统提示中分配的特征做出不同的安全决策，这种现象被称为特征引起的安全变异。以往的方法未能有效管理这种变异，导致安全响应不可靠。提出的方法，特征不变安全调优（TIST），旨在通过将模型的特征条件响应与其无特征行为对齐，来稳定特征间的安全行为，采用自蒸馏框架。该研究引入了特征子空间中和（TraSN），作为TIST的具体实现，确保在通过表示级分析识别的低维特征子空间内的不变性。实验结果表明，TraSN增强了特征不变安全性，提高了对有害请求的响应，同时保持了整体模型能力，从而支持了在LLMs中实现一致安全行为的目标。
ProbGuard: Calibrated Safety Risk Estimation from LLM Output Distributions
Authors: Xinzhe Huang, Biwu Yao, Kedong Xiu, Mengnan Zhao, Di Wang, Puning Zhao, Tianhang Zheng
First: 2026-08-11T08:08:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-11T08:08:41+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Recent research on Large Language Model (LLM) safety has widely adopted guardrails to identify unsafe LLM outputs. Existing guardrails typically formulate safety assessment as a deterministic classification task, mapping a discrete token sequence to a discrete safety label. However, this paradigm has two limitations: First, safety assessment is inherently an uncertain problem, particularly during the early generation state. Second, relying solely on discrete token sequences discards the rich probabilistic information embedded in the LLM output distribution. To address these limitations, we propose the first completely probabilistic architecture-agnostic guardrail \textsc{ProbGuard} to leverage the LLM early output distributional signals for estimating and calibrating the safety probability, thereby enabling early stopping of unsafe ongoing outputs. Specifically, given an LLM's generated prefix distribution, we formulate the safety risk as the unsafe probability of its continued generation dynamics and estimate this risk by Monte-Carlo sampling. Through post-training on the distributional signals and calibrated safety risk, \textsc{ProbGuard} achieves the best calibration performance across all nine model--dataset combination settings, reducing the average Brier score and ECE by 79.6\% and 71.9\%, respectively, over the best baseline. \textsc{ProbGuard} further limits the attack success rate to at most 1\% across six representative jailbreak attacks after observing the LLM early output distributions from only the first ten decoding steps.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ProbGuard：基于LLM输出分布的校准安全风险估计
近期对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的研究广泛采用了保护措施来识别不安全的LLM输出。现有的保护措施通常将安全评估表述为一个确定性的分类任务，将离散的标记序列映射到离散的安全标签。然而，这一范式存在两个局限性：首先，安全评估本质上是一个不确定性问题，特别是在早期生成阶段。其次，仅依赖离散标记序列会丢弃嵌入在LLM输出分布中的丰富概率信息。为了解决这些局限性，我们提出了第一个完全概率架构无关的保护措施\textsc{ProbGuard}，利用LLM早期输出分布信号来估计和校准安全概率，从而实现对不安全持续输出的早期停止。具体而言，给定LLM生成的前缀分布，我们将安全风险表述为其持续生成动态的不安全概率，并通过蒙特卡洛采样来估计这一风险。通过对分布信号和校准安全风险的后训练，\textsc{ProbGuard}在所有九个模型-数据集组合设置中实现了最佳的校准性能，平均Brier分数和ECE分别减少了79.6\%和71.9\%，超越了最佳基线。\textsc{ProbGuard}进一步将六个代表性越狱攻击的攻击成功率限制在最多1\%以内，仅在观察到LLM早期输出分布的前十个解码步骤后。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of existing safety assessment methods for Large Language Models (LLMs), which typically treat safety as a deterministic classification task, neglecting the inherent uncertainty and rich probabilistic information in LLM outputs. The proposed approach, ProbGuard, differs by introducing a probabilistic architecture-agnostic guardrail that utilizes early output distribution signals to estimate and calibrate safety probabilities, allowing for early termination of unsafe outputs. This method is well-motivated as it directly tackles the uncertainty in safety assessments. The contribution of the paper lies in its innovative use of Monte-Carlo sampling to estimate safety risks based on LLM output distributions, achieving significant improvements in calibration performance, with a 79.6% reduction in average Brier score and a 71.9% reduction in expected calibration error across various model-dataset combinations. Additionally, ProbGuard effectively limits the attack success rate to 1% in six jailbreak scenarios after analyzing the initial decoding steps of the LLM.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）安全评估方法的局限性，这些方法通常将安全评估视为确定性分类任务，忽视了LLM输出中固有的不确定性和丰富的概率信息。所提出的方法ProbGuard引入了一种概率架构无关的保护措施，利用LLM的早期输出分布信号来估计和校准安全概率，从而允许在输出不安全时提前终止。本文的贡献在于其创新的方法论，采用蒙特卡洛采样根据LLM生成的前缀分布评估安全风险。ProbGuard在各种模型-数据集组合中表现出显著的性能提升，平均Brier分数降低了79.6%，期望校准误差降低了71.9%，同时在代表性越狱场景中将攻击成功率限制在1%，有效支持其安全目标。
Agent Safety Should Be a Runtime Contract
Authors: Albus W. Ng, Yi Han, Jusheng Zhang, Wenhao Wang
First: 2026-08-11T08:01:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-11T08:01:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The dominant paradigm treats AI safety as a property to be instilled during model training via RLHF, DPO, or Constitutional AI. We argue this is structurally insufficient for autonomous agents that execute code, mutate files, send messages, and modify databases. Agent safety should be a runtime contract enforced by the harness, and the contract has two complementary faces. The preventive face blocks dangerous actions before they happen via sandboxes, permission gates, output filters, and trajectory monitors. The evidential face requires verifiable proof that good actions actually happened, gating task submission on hard evidence such as test runs, log captures, file diffs, and citation grounding. We ground the position in four lines of public evidence, with row-level protocols and data released in the supplementary JSON files: a survey of 52 documented AI-agent and LLM safety incidents, a false-completion audit with 31 non-contested core cases plus one disputed illustrative case, a trajectory-schema audit of 12 public agent systems and harnesses, and a title-level audit of all 28,560 papers accepted at NeurIPS, ICML, and ICLR 2023-2025 showing a pooled 8-12x imbalance between training-time and deployment-time publication. Two prior communities that needed to enforce safety, computer security and the experimental sciences, converged on runtime contracts with both preventive and evidential elements; agentic AI is now under the same pressure. We formalize an Agent Trajectory Schema and Evidence Chain, state a compositional gating proposition based on standard monitor composition, and outline a research agenda. The right unit of safety in agentic AI is the trajectory-with-checkable-evidence, not the model.
中文标题/摘要
标题：代理安全应为运行时契约
主流范式将AI安全视为在模型训练期间通过RLHF、DPO或宪法AI灌输的属性。我们认为这对于执行代码、修改文件、发送消息和修改数据库的自主代理来说结构上是不够的。代理安全应由安全框架强制执行的运行时契约，该契约有两个互补的方面。预防性方面通过沙箱、权限门、输出过滤器和轨迹监控在危险行为发生之前阻止它们。证据性方面要求可验证的证据证明良好行为确实发生，任务提交需基于硬证据，如测试运行、日志捕获、文件差异和引用基础。我们在四条公共证据中支持这一立场，附加JSON文件中发布了行级协议和数据：对52起记录的AI代理和LLM安全事件的调查、31个无争议核心案例加一个有争议示例案例的虚假完成审计、12个公共代理系统和安全框架的轨迹模式审计，以及对2023-2025年NeurIPS、ICML和ICLR接受的28,560篇论文的标题级审计，显示训练时间和部署时间出版之间的8-12倍不平衡。两个需要强制执行安全的先前社区，计算机安全和实验科学，已在预防性和证据性元素的运行时契约上达成共识；代理AI现在也面临同样的压力。我们形式化了代理轨迹模式和证据链，基于标准监控组合提出了一个组合门控命题，并概述了研究议程。在代理AI中，安全的正确单位是带可检查证据的轨迹，而不是模型。
Summary / 总结
This paper addresses the inadequacy of current AI safety paradigms, which primarily focus on instilling safety during model training through methods like RLHF and DPO. These approaches are insufficient for autonomous agents that perform complex actions such as executing code and modifying databases. The authors propose that agent safety should be treated as a runtime contract, incorporating both preventive measures to block dangerous actions and evidential requirements to verify that safe actions have occurred. The paper contributes by presenting an Agent Trajectory Schema and Evidence Chain, supported by empirical evidence from various audits and surveys of AI safety incidents. The proposed methodology emphasizes the importance of checkable evidence in ensuring safety during the deployment of agentic AI, suggesting a shift in focus from training-time safety to runtime safety, which is crucial for effective performance in real-world applications.
本文探讨了当前AI安全范式的不足之处，这些范式主要集中在通过RLHF和DPO等方法在模型训练期间灌输安全性。这些方法对于执行代码和修改数据库等操作的自主代理而言是不够的。作者提出，代理安全应视为一种运行时契约，包括防止危险行为的措施和验证安全行为执行的证据要求。本文的贡献在于提出了一个正式的代理轨迹模式和证据链，以及一个组合门控命题，并概述了增强代理AI安全性的研究议程。所提方法强调在部署过程中可检查证据的重要性，研究结果表明，安全相关研究在训练和部署阶段的出版存在显著不平衡，表明在实际应用中需要改进安全措施。
HoloAegis: Frozen Representation, Topological Inference: Minimally Parametric Safety Manifolds for Zero-Shot LLM Guardrails
Authors: Tak Ho Alex Li, Kaijie Liu, Lik-Hang Lee, Kin Chung Ho, Ping Shum, Michael K. Ng
First: 2026-08-09T05:05:12+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-09T05:05:12+00:00
Comments: Preprint, August 2026. 10 tables, 2 figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Current LLM safety guardrails face a fundamental tension: fine-tuning distorts pre-trained representations while generative judges incur prohibitive inference costs. We challenge the prevailing paradigm by asking: can safety be achieved through pure geometric reasoning over frozen semantic representations? We present HoloAegis, a minimally parametric topological inference framework that decouples representation from reasoning. We term our approach minimally parametric because the only free parameters are the anchor count K and the temperature tau, both fixed after construction and requiring no gradient-based training. An un-fine-tuned encoder maps text to a unit sphere, after which all decisions are purely geometric. We formalize safety evaluation as a Gibbs-Boltzmann Free Energy computation over a pre-computed System Topology Anchor Bank, and we introduce Dual Time-Scale Exponential Moving Averages to detect progressive multi-turn semantic drift. Our key theoretical insight is a Topological Boundary Stability Conjecture: we provide theoretical motivation and strong empirical evidence that sparse anchor centroids stabilize the decision boundary against high-frequency lexical perturbations far better than full vector space methods. Evaluated across 8 benchmarks, HoloAegis achieves state-of-the-art accuracy (1.0000 AUC on AuthenHallu, 0.9802 on HarmBench) with sub-millisecond latency, zero cold-start data, and cross-lingual transfer (0.9758 AUC on Chinese CHIFRAUD).
中文标题/摘要
标题：HoloAegis：冻结表示、拓扑推理：零样本LLM安全防护的最小参数安全流形
当前LLM安全防护面临根本性矛盾：微调扭曲了预训练表示，而生成性判断则产生了高昂的推理成本。我们通过提出一个问题来挑战现有范式：是否可以通过对冻结语义表示的纯几何推理来实现安全？我们提出HoloAegis，一个最小参数的拓扑推理框架，将表示与推理解耦。我们称这种方法为最小参数，因为唯一的自由参数是锚点数量K和温度tau，构建后均为固定值且无需基于梯度的训练。未微调的编码器将文本映射到单位球体，之后所有决策均为纯几何。我们将安全评估形式化为对预计算系统拓扑锚库的Gibbs-Boltzmann自由能计算，并引入双时间尺度指数移动平均来检测渐进的多轮语义漂移。我们的关键理论见解是拓扑边界稳定性猜想：我们提供了理论动机和强有力的实证证据，表明稀疏锚心在抵御高频词汇扰动方面比全向量空间方法更能稳定决策边界。在8个基准测试中评估，HoloAegis实现了最先进的准确率（AuthenHallu上1.0000 AUC，HarmBench上0.9802），具有亚毫秒延迟、零冷启动数据和跨语言迁移（在中文CHIFRAUD上0.9758 AUC）。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the challenge of ensuring safety in large language models (LLMs) without compromising the integrity of pre-trained representations or incurring high inference costs associated with generative judges. Previous methods often relied on fine-tuning, which distorts these representations, or complex generative models that are computationally expensive. The proposed HoloAegis framework introduces a minimally parametric topological inference approach that maintains frozen semantic representations, allowing for geometric reasoning without gradient-based training. This method is motivated by the need for stability in decision boundaries against lexical perturbations and is validated through theoretical insights and empirical evidence. HoloAegis demonstrates superior performance across eight benchmarks, achieving state-of-the-art accuracy with minimal latency and no cold-start data, thus effectively supporting its goals of enhancing LLM safety while maintaining efficiency.
本研究解决了当前大型语言模型（LLM）安全防护措施的局限性，这些措施通常通过微调损害预训练表示，或在生成评判时产生高昂的推理成本。提出的方法HoloAegis引入了一种最小参数的拓扑推理框架，利用冻结的语义表示，从而避免了传统方法所面临的问题。该方法的动机在于需要一种更高效和稳定的安全评估过程，仅依赖几何推理而不进行基于梯度的训练。HoloAegis通过Gibbs-Boltzmann自由能计算形式化安全评估，并采用技术检测语义漂移，证明稀疏锚心提供了对词汇变化的更高稳定性。该框架在八个基准测试中实现了最先进的性能，包括AuthenHallu上的1.0000 AUC和HarmBench上的0.9802，具有极低的延迟和有效的跨语言迁移，支持其在没有传统方法缺陷的情况下增强LLM安全性的目标。
Yesterday's Shield, Today's Spear: A Self-Evolving Safety Guardrail in Production
Authors: Cong Ming, Jingyi Chen, Bin Liu, Qi Chu, Tao Gong, Nenghai Yu, Yingfei Xiang
First: 2026-08-09T04:31:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-09T04:31:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Deployed LLM safety guardrails are predominantly static: trained once and frozen at release, while new jailbreak techniques and previously un-addressed harmful categories emerge within days, leaving the defense perpetually a step behind. We present SESG (Self-Evolving Safety Guardrails), a multi-agent system running in production. SESG monitors the live traffic behind a deployed guardrail and surfaces two classes of failure: jailbreaks novel in form and harmful categories novel in content. Once a failure is confirmed, a generation agent synthesizes paired training data targeted at it; a validation agent rebalances the batch toward the direction in which the deployed model errs, so that the model's own mistakes steer its training set; and a routing agent matches the training action to the diagnosed gap and returns the next version to production. Over six rounds of live evolution (V0 to V6), a 1.7B guardrail adapts to a new threat in 16-24 hours, with about 2 hours of human effort, versus the 40-90 hours of the manual process it replaces. On six emerging threats, it outperforms static guardrails from 0.6B to 9B and an adaptive baseline while preserving its general screening competence. Since April 2026, SESG has been the primary update pipeline of Sangfor's guardrail, autonomously closing 14 of 15 new threat scenarios in two months. We release 9 test sets for the 6 new threats at https://github.com/Trams1017/SESG. Warning: This paper contains examples that may be harmful or offensive.
中文标题/摘要
标题：昨日的护盾，今日的矛：生产中的自我演化安全护栏
部署的LLM安全护栏主要是静态的：训练一次后在发布时冻结，而新的越狱技术和之前未解决的有害类别在几天内出现，使防御始终滞后。我们提出了SESG（自我演化安全护栏），这是一个在生产中运行的多智能体系统。SESG监控部署护栏后面的实时流量，并揭示两类失败：形式新颖的越狱和内容新颖的有害类别。一旦确认失败，生成代理合成针对该失败的配对训练数据；验证代理将批次重新平衡到部署模型出错的方向，以便模型自身的错误引导其训练集；路由代理将训练动作与诊断出的差距匹配，并将下一个版本返回生产。在六轮实时演化（V0到V6）中，一个1.7B的护栏在16-24小时内适应新威胁，约需2小时的人力，而替代的手动过程需40-90小时。在六个新兴威胁中，它的表现优于从0.6B到9B的静态护栏和自适应基线，同时保持其一般筛选能力。自2026年4月以来，SESG已成为Sangfor护栏的主要更新管道，在两个月内自主关闭15个新威胁场景中的14个。我们在https://github.com/Trams1017/SESG发布了6个新威胁的9个测试集。警告：本文包含可能有害或冒犯的示例。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of static safety guardrails in large language models (LLMs), which quickly become outdated due to emerging jailbreak techniques and harmful content categories. Previous methods relied on manual updates that were time-consuming and inefficient, often taking 40-90 hours to adapt to new threats. The proposed Self-Evolving Safety Guardrails (SESG) system introduces a multi-agent approach that autonomously monitors live traffic and identifies failures, synthesizes targeted training data, and updates the model within 16-24 hours with minimal human intervention. This method significantly enhances the adaptability of safety measures, achieving superior performance against six emerging threats compared to static guardrails and an adaptive baseline, while maintaining general screening capabilities. SESG has been effectively implemented as the primary update mechanism for Sangfor's guardrail, successfully addressing 14 out of 15 new threat scenarios within two months of deployment.

Automating Deception: Scalable Multi-Turn LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Adarsh Kumarappan, Ananya Mujoo
First: 2025-11-24T03:15:11+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-08T15:49:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multi-turn conversational attacks, which leverage psychological principles like Foot-in-the-Door (FITD), where a small initial request paves the way for a more significant one, to bypass safety alignments, pose a persistent threat to Large Language Models (LLMs). Progress in defending against these attacks is hindered by a reliance on manual, hard-to-scale dataset creation. This paper introduces a novel, automated pipeline for generating large-scale, psychologically-grounded multi-turn jailbreak datasets. We systematically operationalize FITD techniques into reproducible templates, creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios across illegal activities and offensive content. We evaluate seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn (with history) and single-turn (without history) conditions. Our results reveal stark differences in contextual robustness: models in the GPT family demonstrate a significant vulnerability to conversational history, with Attack Success Rates (ASR) increasing by as much as 32 percentage points. In contrast, Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash exhibits exceptional resilience, proving nearly immune to these attacks, while Anthropic's Claude 3 Haiku shows strong but imperfect resistance. These findings highlight a critical divergence in how current safety architectures handle conversational context and underscore the need for defenses that can resist narrative-based manipulation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自动化欺骗：可扩展的多轮LLM越狱
多轮对话攻击利用心理学原理，如“脚踏实地”（FITD），通过小的初始请求为更大的请求铺平道路，从而绕过安全对齐，对大型语言模型（LLMs）构成持续威胁。防御这些攻击的进展受到依赖于手动、难以扩展的数据集创建的限制。本文介绍了一种新颖的自动化管道，用于生成大规模、基于心理学的多轮越狱数据集。我们系统地将FITD技术操作化为可重复的模板，创建了一个涵盖非法活动和攻击性内容的1,500个场景的基准。我们在多轮（有历史）和单轮（无历史）条件下评估了来自三个主要LLM家族的七个模型。我们的结果揭示了上下文鲁棒性方面的显著差异：GPT家族的模型对对话历史表现出显著脆弱性，攻击成功率（ASR）增加了多达32个百分点。相比之下，谷歌的Gemini 2.5 Flash表现出卓越的韧性，几乎对这些攻击免疫，而Anthropic的Claude 3 Haiku则显示出强但不完美的抵抗力。这些发现突显了当前安全架构在处理对话上下文方面的关键分歧，并强调了需要能够抵御叙事操控的防御措施。

The research addresses the ongoing threat posed by multi-turn conversational attacks on Large Language Models (LLMs), which exploit psychological principles like the Foot-in-the-Door (FITD) technique to bypass safety measures. Previous methods relied heavily on manual dataset creation, which is not scalable and limits the effectiveness of defenses against these attacks. The proposed approach automates the generation of large-scale, psychologically-informed multi-turn jailbreak datasets, operationalizing FITD techniques into reproducible templates and creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios. CASA: Classification Augmented with Safety Attention for Robust Multimodal Alignment
Authors: Anurag Kumar, Raghuveer Peri, Jon Burnsky, Alexandru Nelus, Rohit Paturi, Srikanth Vishnubhotla, Yanjun Qi
First: 2026-03-31T23:19:50+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-06T22:04:31+00:00
Comments: 9 pages + Appendix section
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multimodal large-language models (MLLMs) often experience degraded safety alignment when harmful queries exploit cross-modal interactions. Models aligned on text alone show a higher rate of successful attacks when extended to two or more modalities. We propose a simple conditional decoding strategy, CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) that uses internal representations of MLLMs to predict a binary safety token before response generation. The classification is guided by a safety attention mechanism that computes attention between a dedicated query prompt and the input to scale the classification logits, sharpening the model's ability to detect malicious queries. Our design ensures robust safety alignment without relying on any external classifier or auxiliary head, and without modality-specific safety fine-tuning. Unlike prior safety-decoding methods that attach an external classifier or moderation head, or rescale every generated token with a reward model, CASA makes a single, internal, attention-gated safety decision over the shared multimodal representation, which is precisely what lets safety learned from text-only training transfer to image and audio inputs. Compared against 4 baseline models across 7 attack types spanning text, image, and audio modalities, evaluated on benchmarks such as MM-SafetyBench, JailbreakV-28k, and adversarial audio tests, CASA lowers the average attack success rate by >90% relative when paired with a standard safety prompt (applied to all baselines for fair comparison), and by 54--61% using its internal safety mechanism alone. Our empirical evaluations also show that CASA~maintains strong utility on benign inputs, validated through both automated and human evaluations (via 13 trained annotators). Together, these results highlight CASA as a simple and generalizable framework to improve multimodal LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CASA：用于稳健多模态对齐的安全注意力增强分类
多模态大语言模型（MLLMs）在有害查询利用跨模态交互时，往往会出现安全对齐下降的问题。仅在文本上对齐的模型在扩展到两个或更多模态时，成功攻击的比率更高。我们提出了一种简单的条件解码策略CASA（安全注意力增强分类），利用MLLMs的内部表示在生成响应之前预测二元安全标记。分类由安全注意力机制引导，该机制计算专用查询提示与输入之间的注意力，以缩放分类logits，增强模型检测恶意查询的能力。我们的设计确保了稳健的安全对齐，而不依赖于任何外部分类器或辅助头，也不需要特定模态的安全微调。与之前附加外部分类器或审核头的安全解码方法不同，CASA在共享的多模态表示上做出单一的内部注意力门控安全决策，这正是使得从仅文本训练中学到的安全性能够转移到图像和音频输入的原因。与4个基线模型在7种攻击类型（涵盖文本、图像和音频模态）进行比较，并在MM-SafetyBench、JailbreakV-28k和对抗音频测试等基准上进行评估，CASA在与标准安全提示（对所有基线进行公平比较）配对时，平均攻击成功率降低了>90%，仅使用其内部安全机制时降低了54%至61%。我们的实证评估还表明，CASA在良性输入上保持强大的实用性，通过13名训练过的注释员进行的自动和人工评估得到了验证。这些结果共同突显了CASA作为一个简单且可推广的框架，以提高多模态LLM的安全性。

Previous methods relied on external classifiers or moderation heads, leading to inefficiencies and limitations in safety across modalities. The proposed CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) approach introduces a conditional decoding strategy that utilizes internal representations to predict a binary safety token, guided by a safety attention mechanism that enhances the model's ability to identify malicious queries without external dependencies. This method contributes to robust safety alignment and demonstrates significant improvements in performance, achieving over 90% reduction in attack success rates across various modalities compared to baseline models, while maintaining strong utility on benign inputs, thus supporting its effectiveness in enhancing multimodal LLM safety.

Item Response Theory for AI Safety
Authors: Joshua Fonseca Rivera, Neil Shah, David Demitri Africa, Konstantinos Voudouris
First: 2026-08-05T17:25:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-05T17:25:27+00:00
Comments: 15 pages, 9 figures, 6 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Language models differ in how safely they behave and these differences are measured by safety benchmarks. But aggregated benchmark scores are hard to trust and interpret, because benchmarks duplicate one another, correlate heavily, and models may sandbag when they detect evaluation. To address these issues, we draw on Item Response Theory (IRT), a statistical toolkit for measuring these latents from performance on items with inferred psychometric properties. We fit IRT models to eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models, the largest psychometric analysis of LLM safety evaluations to date, and contribute three results. First, we find that three interpretable factors of refusal strictness, truthfulness, and contextual harm explain most of the variance between models across benchmarks. Second, psychometrically selected items recover full benchmark scores with lower error than random subsets of the same size, and roughly ten adaptively chosen items suffice for several individual benchmarks, cutting evaluation cost by 97-99%. Third, IRT supports audits of individual models, showing that it can be used to detect naive sandbagging and changes of model behind APIs. Overall, we show IRT is a ready-made toolkit for reading, reducing, and auditing safety benchmarks, which we recommend frontier labs and evaluators adopt.
中文标题/摘要
标题：人工智能安全的项目反应理论
语言模型在安全行为上存在差异，这些差异通过安全基准进行测量。但聚合的基准分数难以信任和解释，因为基准之间存在重复，相关性很高，模型在检测到评估时可能会故意降低表现。为了解决这些问题，我们借鉴了项目反应理论（IRT），这是一种通过对具有推断心理测量特性的项目表现进行测量的统计工具包。我们将IRT模型拟合到192个语言模型的八个安全基准上，这是迄今为止对大型语言模型安全评估的最大心理测量分析，并贡献了三个结果。首先，我们发现拒绝严格性、真实性和上下文伤害这三个可解释因素解释了模型在基准之间的大部分方差。其次，心理测量选择的项目以低于随机子集的误差恢复完整的基准分数，且大约十个自适应选择的项目足以满足几个单独基准，将评估成本降低97-99%。第三，IRT支持对单个模型的审计，表明它可以用于检测幼稚的故意降低表现和API背后模型的变化。总体而言，我们展示了IRT是一个现成的工具包，用于解读、减少和审计安全基准，我们建议前沿实验室和评估者采用。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the challenge of evaluating the safety of language models, which often exhibit varying behaviors that are difficult to interpret due to the redundancy and correlation among existing safety benchmarks. Previous methods have struggled with these issues, leading to unreliable aggregated scores and potential model manipulation during evaluations. The proposed approach utilizes Item Response Theory (IRT) to analyze safety benchmarks, offering a more nuanced understanding of model performance by identifying key factors such as refusal strictness, truthfulness, and contextual harm. The methodology involves fitting IRT models to data from eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models, revealing that a small number of carefully selected items can effectively replace larger benchmark sets, thus significantly reducing evaluation costs. The findings demonstrate that IRT not only enhances the reliability of safety assessments but also facilitates the detection of model behavior anomalies, making it a valuable tool for researchers and evaluators in the field.
本研究解决了测量语言模型安全性中的挑战，现有基准往往由于重复和相关性产生不可靠且难以解释的聚合分数。以往的方法缺乏有效区分模型行为的能力，并且容易受到操控，如故意降低表现。本文提出使用项目反应理论（IRT）作为分析安全基准的新方法，提供了一个更可靠的模型性能评估框架。该方法涉及对192个语言模型的八个安全基准拟合IRT模型，揭示了三个关键因素解释了模型安全性中的差异：拒绝严格性、真实性和上下文危害。研究结果表明，IRT可以在保持准确性的同时将评估成本降低97-99%，并能够对单个模型进行审计以检测操控，从而为改善AI系统的安全评估贡献了一个强有力的工具。
Robust General Utility for Reinforcement Learning
Authors: Zixuan Liu, Fangzheng Wu, Brian Summa, Zizhan Zheng
First: 2026-08-04T12:28:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-04T12:28:39+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Reinforcement learning (RL) with general utility extends classic RL by optimizing an arbitrary utility functional of the policy-induced occupancy measure, thereby enabling a broader range of applications. However, previous work on general utility RL typically assumes the evaluation utility is fixed and correctly specified. In practice, the utility used at deployment can deviate from the training one, creating a robustness gap that prior work does not address. Motivated by this, we propose robust general-utility RL, a minimax learning framework that trains policies against utility misspecification within a prescribed uncertainty set. Our framework strictly generalizes standard general-utility RL while also providing a unified view of many existing RL frameworks, including reward-robust RL and constrained RL, through appropriate choices of the utility uncertainty set. We further develop provably convergent stochastic algorithms for two regimes. For concave utilities, we develop a projected stochastic gradient descent-ascent method and establish stationarity guarantees. For the more challenging nonconcave regime, we propose a stochastic prox-extragradient algorithm that mitigates ill-posed behavior induced by nonconcavity, with convergence guarantees to approximate first-order stationarity. Experiments on LLM safety alignment and exploration maximization tasks further corroborate the convergence behavior consistent with our theory.
中文标题/摘要
标题：强化学习的鲁棒一般效用
具有一般效用的强化学习（RL）通过优化策略引起的占用测度的任意效用泛函，扩展了经典RL，从而使其能够应用于更广泛的场景。然而，以往关于一般效用RL的研究通常假设评估效用是固定且正确指定的。在实践中，部署时使用的效用可能与训练时的效用有所偏离，造成了以往研究未能解决的鲁棒性差距。基于此，我们提出了鲁棒一般效用RL，这是一种最小最大学习框架，旨在针对规定的不确定性集内的效用误指定训练策略。我们的框架严格推广了标准的一般效用RL，同时通过适当选择效用不确定性集，提供了许多现有RL框架（包括奖励鲁棒RL和约束RL）的统一视角。我们进一步为两种情况开发了可证明收敛的随机算法。对于凹效用，我们开发了一种投影随机梯度下降-上升方法，并建立了平稳性保证。对于更具挑战性的非凹情况，我们提出了一种随机近似外梯度算法，减轻了非凹性引起的不良行为，并保证收敛以近似一阶平稳性。在LLM安全对齐和探索最大化任务上的实验进一步证实了与我们理论一致的收敛行为。
Summary / 总结
This paper addresses the limitations of traditional reinforcement learning (RL) methods that optimize a fixed utility functional, which can lead to a robustness gap when the utility at deployment differs from that used during training. The proposed robust general-utility RL introduces a minimax learning framework that accounts for utility misspecification by training policies within a defined uncertainty set, thus generalizing existing RL frameworks and improving robustness. The contribution lies in the development of stochastic algorithms for both concave and nonconcave utility functions, ensuring convergence and addressing challenges posed by nonconcavity. The methodology demonstrates effectiveness through experiments on tasks related to large language model safety alignment and exploration maximization, achieving performance that supports the proposed goals of robustness and generalization in RL applications.
本文解决了传统强化学习（RL）方法的局限性，这些方法优化固定的效用函数，当部署时的效用与训练期间使用的效用不同，可能导致鲁棒性差距。所提出的鲁棒一般效用RL框架引入了一种极小极大学习方法，考虑了在定义的不确定性集合内的效用错误指定，从而推广了现有的RL框架并增强了其适用性。该论文的贡献在于开发了确保收敛的随机算法，针对凹和非凹效用函数分别设计了具体的方法。通过在LLM安全对齐和探索最大化任务上的实验验证了该方法论，显示出改进的性能，并支持该框架在效用错误指定下的鲁棒性。
EduZone: A Framework for Evaluating LLM Safety for K-12 Students and Teachers
Authors: Junyeong Park, Jieun Han, Haneul Yoo, So-Yeon Ahn, Jinsung Yoon, Alice Oh
First: 2026-08-03T10:23:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-03T10:23:42+00:00
Comments: Under Review
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used across diverse tasks in K-12 education, yet existing safety evaluations rarely examine how harmful or inappropriate content appears in interactions between LLMs and students or teachers. To address this, we present EduZone, an evaluation framework for LLM safety across diverse educational scenarios. Our framework systematically combines (1) student- and teacher-facing LLM usage contexts, (2) fine-grained curriculum concepts, and (3) 6 risk categories and 28 subcategories spanning both conventional and education-specific harms to generate contextually grounded adversarial interactions. We construct these interactions in three settings: single-turn requests, static multi-turn conversations, and dynamic multi-turn conversations. Using these interactions, we evaluate ten LLMs using four safety levels: refusal, safe assistance, risky assistance with safety guidance, and fully risky assistance. Our results reveal greater vulnerability to education-specific risks and dynamic multi-turn interactions, while existing safety guardrails fail to adequately address these risks. EduZone advances LLM safety in education by providing an automated, scalable evaluation framework that supports the development and deployment of safer LLMs in K-12 education.
中文标题/摘要
标题：EduZone：评估K-12学生和教师LLM安全性的框架
大型语言模型（LLMs）在K-12教育中被广泛应用于各种任务，但现有的安全评估很少考察LLMs与学生或教师之间的互动中出现的有害或不当内容。为了解决这个问题，我们提出了EduZone，一个针对不同教育场景的LLM安全评估框架。我们的框架系统地结合了（1）面向学生和教师的LLM使用背景，（2）细化的课程概念，以及（3）涵盖传统和教育特定危害的6个风险类别和28个子类别，以生成具有情境基础的对抗性互动。我们在三种设置中构建这些互动：单轮请求、静态多轮对话和动态多轮对话。利用这些互动，我们使用四个安全级别评估十个LLMs：拒绝、安全协助、带安全指导的风险协助和完全风险协助。我们的结果揭示了对教育特定风险和动态多轮互动的更大脆弱性，而现有的安全防护措施未能充分应对这些风险。EduZone通过提供一个自动化、可扩展的评估框架，推动了教育中LLM的安全性，支持更安全的LLM在K-12教育中的开发和部署。

Single Canonical Prompts Underestimate LLM Safety's Surface-Form Sensitivity
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Junwei Yao, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Wenbo Ye, Jiaxi Wen, Lai Yun Choi
First: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00
Comments: 9 pages, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
A benchmark score is a measurement instrument, yet most benchmarks read each item at a single canonical surface form. We ask whether that reading is faithful: when an item's intent is held fixed and only its meaning-preserving surface form varies, does the canonical-form score estimate model behavior well, and how much of any variation is decoding/judge noise rather than signal? We instantiate this in safety, a high-stakes setting with no gold label to average toward. To avoid prior confounds, we pre-author the reformulations (refusal-free, mostly non-LLM: machine back-translation and a Matrix-Language-Frame code-switch generator) so an identical surface form reaches every model, score all responses with one human-anchored, vendor-neutral judge (Claude, kappa = 0.86 vs. human on unsafe compliance, stable across languages, cross-checked by GPT-4o), and verify intent preservation. On 370 seeds x 5 surface forms x 5 models, no single transformation is uniformly most dangerous (6 of 20 per-transformation McNemar tests survive correction, most protective). Yet evaluating only the canonical prompt underestimates unsafe compliance: the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds even the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 pp, with bootstrap 95% CIs excluding zero for all five models, and 5-13% of seeds safe on canonical are unsafe under some reformulation -- above a zero stochasticity floor (canonical resampled five times at temperature 0 gives 0/370 new exposures). The size of this gap is model-dependent (largest on Gemini 2.5 Pro). One form recovers only ~53% of a model's observed unsafe surface and about three reach 85% -- a redundancy characterization of this form set, not of a defined population. A benign control (XSTest) suggests the instability is bidirectional, though the benign and harmful pools are not item-matched. We release the dataset, code, and per-response labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：单一规范提示低估了大型语言模型安全性的表面形式敏感性
基准分数是一种测量工具，但大多数基准以单一规范表面形式读取每个项目。我们询问这种读取是否忠实：当一个项目的意图保持不变，仅其保持意义的表面形式变化时，规范形式分数是否能很好地估计模型行为，以及任何变化中有多少是解码/评判噪声而非信号？我们在安全性这一高风险环境中实例化这一点，因其没有金标准可供平均。为了避免先前的混淆，我们预先授权了重构（无拒绝，主要非大型语言模型：机器回译和矩阵语言框架代码切换生成器），以便相同的表面形式到达每个模型，所有响应由一个人类锚定的、中立的评审（Claude，kappa = 0.86与人类在不安全合规性上的比较，跨语言稳定，由GPT-4o交叉检查）进行评分，并验证意图保持。在370个种子x 5个表面形式x 5个模型中，没有单一变换是普遍最危险的（20个每变换的McNemar测试中有6个在校正后存活，大多数是保护性的）。然而，仅评估规范提示低估了不安全合规性：跨形式的不安全结果的联合超过了最糟糕的单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点，所有五个模型的自助法95%置信区间均不包括零，且5-13%的在规范下安全的种子在某些重构下是不安全的——高于零随机性底线（规范在温度0下重采样五次给出0/370个新暴露）。这一差距的大小依赖于模型（在Gemini 2.5 Pro上最大）。一种形式仅恢复了模型观察到的不安全表面的约53%，大约三种形式达到了85%——这是对这一形式集的冗余特征，而非定义人口的特征。一个良性对照（XSTest）表明不稳定性是双向的，尽管良性和有害池并未按项目匹配。我们发布了数据集、代码和每个响应的标签。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing benchmark evaluations in assessing the safety of large language models (LLMs), which typically rely on a single canonical surface form for scoring. Previous methods often overlook the variability in model behavior that can arise from different surface forms, leading to an underestimation of unsafe compliance. The proposed approach involves pre-authoring multiple reformulations of prompts to ensure that each model receives identical surface forms, thereby allowing for a more accurate assessment of safety across various transformations. The study employs a rigorous methodology involving 370 seeds and five surface forms across five models, revealing that evaluating only the canonical prompt significantly underestimates unsafe outcomes. The findings indicate that the union of unsafe results across different forms surpasses the worst-case scenario of any single form, with model-dependent variations in safety performance, thus contributing valuable insights into the sensitivity of LLM safety assessments and releasing a dataset for further research.
本研究探讨了现有基准评分在评估大型语言模型（LLMs）时的局限性，质疑单一规范表面形式在估计模型行为时的可信度，特别是在没有金标准的安全背景下。以往的方法依赖于固定的表面形式，这可能无法准确反映模型性能，因为响应中存在噪声和变异。提出的方法涉及预先编写多个提示的改写，以确保意图一致，同时变化表面形式，从而对不同模型的安全合规性进行更全面的评估。该研究采用系统的方法，涉及370个种子、五种表面形式和五个模型，揭示仅依赖规范提示显著低估了不安全结果，跨表面形式的不安全结果的联合超过了最差单一形式，差距显著。这一贡献强调了在安全评估中考虑表面形式敏感性的必要性，并提供了未来研究的数据集和方法论。
Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming: Automated Generation of High-Quality and Diverse Attackers for Large Language Models
Authors: Ren-Jian Wang, Ke Xue, Zeyu Qin, Ziniu Li, Sheng Tang, Hao-Tian Li, Shengcai Liu, Zhi Yu, Yuanpeng Tan, Chao Qian
First: 2025-06-08T13:07:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-01T10:21:24+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Ensuring the safety and robustness of large language models (LLMs) is a fundamental challenge and a critical prerequisite for the responsible deployment of artificial intelligence. Red-teaming, a systematic framework to identify adversarial prompts that elicit harmful responses from target LLMs, has emerged as a crucial safety evaluation paradigm. Within this framework, the diversity of adversarial prompts is critical for a comprehensive safety assessment. However, previous red-teaming approaches often pursue diversity through simplistic metrics such as word frequency or sentence embedding similarity, which may not capture meaningful variation in attack strategies. In addition, the common practice of training a single attacker model restricts coverage across all potential attack styles and risk categories. This paper introduces Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming (QDRT), a new framework designed to address these limitations. QDRT achieves goal-driven diversity through behavior-conditioned training and implements a behavioral replay buffer in an open-ended manner. In addition, it trains multiple specialized attackers capable of generating high-quality attacks across diverse styles and risk categories. Our empirical evaluation demonstrates that QDRT generates attacks that are both more diverse and more effective against a wide range of target LLMs, including open-source models \texttt{GPT-2}, \texttt{Llama-3}, \texttt{Gemma-2}, \texttt{Qwen2.5}, and commercial models \texttt{GPT-4.1} and \texttt{GPT-5-Chat}. This work advances the field of LLM safety by providing a systematic and effective approach to automated red-teaming, ultimately supporting the responsible deployment of LLMs. Our code is available at https://github.com/lamda-bbo/QDRT.

标题：质量-多样性红队：大型语言模型高质量和多样化攻击者的自动生成
确保大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全性和稳健性是一个基本挑战，也是负责任地部署人工智能的关键前提。红队是一种系统框架，用于识别引发目标LLMs有害响应的对抗性提示，已成为重要的安全评估范式。在此框架内，对抗性提示的多样性对于全面的安全评估至关重要。然而，以往的红队方法往往通过简单的指标（如词频或句子嵌入相似性）追求多样性，这可能无法捕捉攻击策略的有意义变化。此外，训练单一攻击者模型的常见做法限制了对所有潜在攻击风格和风险类别的覆盖。本文介绍了质量-多样性红队（QDRT），这是一个旨在解决这些局限性的新框架。QDRT通过行为条件训练实现目标驱动的多样性，并以开放式方式实现行为重放缓冲区。此外，它训练多个专门的攻击者，能够在多样化风格和风险类别中生成高质量的攻击。我们的实证评估表明，QDRT生成的攻击在多样性和针对广泛目标LLMs（包括开源模型\texttt{GPT-2}、\texttt{Llama-3}、\texttt{Gemma-2}、\texttt{Qwen2.5}以及商业模型\texttt{GPT-4.1}和\texttt{GPT-5-Chat}）的有效性上均更为出色。这项工作通过提供一种系统有效的自动化红队方法，推动了LLM安全领域的发展，最终支持LLMs的负责任部署。我们的代码可在https://github.com/lamda-bbo/QDRT获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical challenge of ensuring the safety and robustness of large language models (LLMs), which is essential for their responsible deployment. Previous red-teaming methods have relied on simplistic metrics for diversity, such as word frequency, which fail to capture meaningful variations in attack strategies, and typically utilize a single attacker model that limits the exploration of diverse attack styles. The proposed Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming (QDRT) framework overcomes these limitations by employing behavior-conditioned training and a behavioral replay buffer, allowing for the training of multiple specialized attackers that generate high-quality and diverse attacks. This paper contributes to LLM safety by systematically improving automated red-teaming, demonstrating through empirical evaluation that QDRT produces more diverse and effective attacks against various target LLMs, thereby supporting the goal of enhancing LLM safety.

EvalSafetyGap: A Hybrid Survey and Conceptual Framework for LLM Evaluation-Safety Failures
Authors: Buğra Alperen Uluırmak, Rifat Kurban
First: 2026-06-29T12:33:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-31T12:14:44+00:00
Comments: 74 pages, 2 figures, 4 tables. Hybrid systematic survey and conceptual framework on LLM evaluation and AI-safety failures, synthesizing 373 primary studies (2018-2026). Introduces the EvalSafetyGap framework (Instability Decomposition, Alignment Trilemma) and reports an exploratory ten-model audit. Submitted as a review/survey article; not currently under consideration elsewhere
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
This paper presents a systematic survey and conceptual synthesis of the shared measurement problem underlying large language model (LLM) evaluation and AI safety: benchmark scores, reward signals, and safety metrics can improve while the capabilities and alignment properties they are meant to represent remain uncertain. Synthesizing 373 primary studies published between 2018 and 2026, the survey organizes evidence on benchmark validity, contamination, dynamic evaluation, LLM-as-a-judge protocols, adversarial safety testing, reward and proxy optimization, mechanistic interpretability, and AI governance into an eight-stream evidence taxonomy. Building on this synthesis, we introduce EvalSafetyGap, a conceptual framework that unifies benchmark-validity and alignment-failure research as a shared proxy-target divergence problem under optimization pressure, formalized through a Goodhart-inspired Instability Decomposition and an Alignment Trilemma. An exploratory ten-model public-evidence audit illustrates the framework by showing why capability, behavioral robustness, and governance disclosure should be reported as separate evidence layers rather than collapsed into a single safety score. The survey closes with a research agenda for dynamic and contamination-resistant benchmarks, pre-specified multi-attempt threat models, version-locked evaluation, transparent source reporting, and validated mechanistic safety indicators, offering researchers, model developers, and AI auditors a shared vocabulary for measurement-aware LLM safety evaluation.

标题：EvalSafetyGap：大型语言模型评估安全失败的混合调查与概念框架
本文系统性地调查和概念性综合了大型语言模型（LLM）评估和人工智能安全的共享测量问题：基准分数、奖励信号和安全指标可以改善，而它们所代表的能力和对齐属性仍然不确定。通过综合2018年至2026年间发表的373项主要研究，调查将基准有效性、污染、动态评估、LLM作为评判者的协议、对抗性安全测试、奖励和代理优化、机制可解释性以及人工智能治理的证据组织成八个证据分类流。在此基础上，我们引入了EvalSafetyGap，一个概念框架，将基准有效性和对齐失败研究统一为在优化压力下的共享代理-目标偏差问题，通过受Goodhart启发的不稳定性分解和对齐三难问题进行形式化。一个探索性的十模型公共证据审计通过展示能力、行为鲁棒性和治理披露为何应作为单独的证据层报告，而不是合并为单一安全分数，来说明该框架。调查最后提出了一个研究议程，涉及动态和抗污染基准、预先指定的多次尝试威胁模型、版本锁定评估、透明的来源报告和验证的机制安全指标，为研究人员、模型开发者和人工智能审计师提供了一个共享的测量意识的LLM安全评估词汇。

The research addresses the critical issue of evaluating large language models (LLMs) in the context of AI safety, highlighting the uncertainty in benchmark scores and safety metrics despite improvements. Previous methods have struggled with benchmark validity and the contamination of evaluation processes, leading to misalignment between reported capabilities and actual performance. The proposed EvalSafetyGap framework offers a novel approach by framing these issues as a shared proxy-target divergence problem, utilizing concepts like Instability Decomposition and the Alignment Trilemma to better understand and address these challenges. 本研究解决了评估大型语言模型（LLM）和确保人工智能安全的挑战，强调基准分数和安全指标的改善并不保证与预期能力的一致性。以往的方法在基准有效性和安全分数混淆等问题上存在困难，导致模型评估的不确定性。提出的EvalSafetyGap框架通过将评估问题框定为优化压力下的代理-目标偏差，利用不稳定性分解和对齐三难问题等概念，展现出其独特性。这种方法具有良好的动机，因为它提供了一种结构化的方式来分析和报告LLM安全性，强调能力、鲁棒性和治理的独立证据层的重要性。该方法论包括对373项研究的系统调查和对十个模型的探索性审计，展示了框架的适用性，并提出了开发更可靠基准和评估实践的研究议程。
On-Policy Distillation for LLM Safety: A Routing Approach to Template-Robust Realignment
Authors: Yongjian Guo, Wanlun Ma, Lingyu Shen, Xi Xiao, Sheng Wen
First: 2026-07-29T16:07:19+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-29T16:07:19+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Fine-tuning is the dominant paradigm for specializing large language models (LLMs), yet it exposes a critical vulnerability: malicious data providers can embed harmful behaviors into downstream corpora, creating models that retain professional skills while violating human values on demand. Existing safety-realignment defenses often fail in practice due to three key limitations: they frequently cause catastrophic forgetting of specialized skills; their effectiveness collapses when the defender cannot observe the attacker's prompt template; and successfully realigned models remain susceptible to re-jailbreaking via simple system prompt switches. To address these challenges, we propose Routing-based On-Policy Distillation (ROPD), a novel realignment framework that models the divergence between aligned and compromised output probability distributions rather than fitting specific prompt templates. We conduct extensive experiments comparing ROPD against four state-of-the-art baselines across three datasets and three base models with varying alignment strengths. Our results demonstrate that when baseline defenses face template mismatches, often accompanied by severe degradation in downstream task performance. In contrast, ROPD substantially mitigates template-mismatch risks, maintaining superior robustness in both defense effectiveness and capability preservation. While our analysis indicates ROPD is not entirely immune to template shifts, its performance degradation is negligible compared to existing methods, establishing a new standard for robust LLM realignment.
中文标题/摘要
标题：基于策略蒸馏的LLM安全性：一种模板鲁棒重对齐的路由方法
微调是专门化大型语言模型（LLMs）的主流范式，但它暴露了一个关键漏洞：恶意数据提供者可以将有害行为嵌入下游语料库，创建在需求下保留专业技能但违反人类价值观的模型。现有的安全重对齐防御在实践中常常失败，主要有三个关键限制：它们经常导致专业技能的灾难性遗忘；当防御者无法观察攻击者的提示模板时，其有效性崩溃；成功重对齐的模型仍然容易通过简单的系统提示切换重新越狱。为了解决这些挑战，我们提出了基于路由的策略蒸馏（ROPD），这是一种新颖的重对齐框架，它建模对齐和妥协输出概率分布之间的差异，而不是拟合特定的提示模板。我们进行了广泛的实验，将ROPD与四个最先进的基线在三个数据集和三个基础模型上进行比较，基线模型具有不同的对齐强度。我们的结果表明，当基线防御面临模板不匹配时，通常伴随着下游任务性能的严重下降。相比之下，ROPD显著减轻了模板不匹配的风险，在防御有效性和能力保留方面保持了优越的鲁棒性。尽管我们的分析表明ROPD并非完全免疫于模板变化，但其性能下降与现有方法相比微不足道，为鲁棒的LLM重对齐建立了新的标准。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerabilities of large language models (LLMs) during fine-tuning, particularly the risks posed by malicious data that can embed harmful behaviors. Previous methods for safety realignment often lead to catastrophic forgetting of specialized skills, struggle with unobservable attack templates, and remain vulnerable to re-jailbreaking. The proposed Routing-based On-Policy Distillation (ROPD) framework differs by focusing on the divergence between aligned and compromised output distributions instead of specific templates, effectively addressing these limitations. The paper contributes a novel methodology that demonstrates ROPD's robustness against template mismatches, achieving superior performance in defense effectiveness and capability preservation compared to four state-of-the-art baselines across multiple datasets and models. The results indicate that ROPD significantly reduces risks associated with template mismatches while maintaining high performance in downstream tasks, supporting its goals of enhancing LLM safety.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLM）在微调过程中的脆弱性，特别是恶意数据提供者可能引入有害行为的风险。以往的安全重对齐方法常常导致专业技能的灾难性遗忘，难以处理未观察到的攻击模板，并且仍然容易受到重新监禁的影响。提出的基于路由的在线蒸馏（ROPD）框架通过关注对齐和妥协输出分布之间的差异，而不是特定模板，来改善这些局限性。这种方法具有良好的动机，旨在增强防御效果和能力保留。实验结果表明，ROPD在多个数据集和模型上优于四种最先进的基线，显著降低了与模板不匹配相关的风险，同时保持了强大的性能，从而为LLM重对齐建立了新的标准。
MEDIC: Comprehensive Evaluation of Leading Indicators for LLM Safety and Utility in Clinical Applications
Authors: Praveenkumar Kanithi, Clément Christophe, Marco AF Pimentel, Tathagata Raha, Prateek Munjal, Nada Saadi, Hamza A Javed, Svetlana Maslenkova, Nasir Hayat, Ronnie Rajan, Shadab Khan
First: 2024-09-11T14:44:51+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-28T17:58:21+00:00
Comments: Published in Transactions on Machine Learning Research (06/2026)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
While Large Language Models (LLMs) achieve superhuman performance on standardized medical licensing exams, these static benchmarks have become saturated and increasingly disconnected from the functional requirements of clinical workflows. To bridge the gap between theoretical capability and verified utility, we introduce MEDIC, a comprehensive evaluation framework establishing leading indicators of clinical LLM competence across five dimensions. These upfront indicators reveal cross-benchmark capability gaps, such as the divergence between static knowledge retrieval and functional execution, that inform model selection before costly deployment-based evaluation. Beyond standard question-answering, we assess operational capabilities using deterministic execution protocols and a novel Cross-Examination Framework (CEF), which quantifies information fidelity and hallucination rates without reliance on reference texts. Our evaluation across a heterogeneous task suite exposes critical performance trade-offs: we identify a significant knowledge-execution gap, where proficiency in static retrieval does not predict success in operational tasks such as clinical calculation or SQL generation. Furthermore, we observe a divergence between passive safety (refusal) and active safety (error detection), revealing that models fine-tuned for high refusal rates often fail to reliably audit clinical documentation for factual accuracy. These findings demonstrate that no single architecture dominates across all dimensions, highlighting the necessity of a portfolio approach to clinical model deployment. We accompany this work with a publicly available MEDIC leaderboard at https://hf.co/spaces/m42-health/MEDIC-Benchmark.
中文标题/摘要
标题：MEDIC：临床应用中大型语言模型安全性和实用性的领先指标综合评估
尽管大型语言模型（LLMs）在标准化医学执照考试中表现出超人类的能力，但这些静态基准已趋于饱和，并与临床工作流程的功能需求日益脱节。为弥合理论能力与验证实用性之间的差距，我们引入了MEDIC，一个综合评估框架，建立了临床LLM能力的领先指标，涵盖五个维度。这些前期指标揭示了跨基准能力差距，例如静态知识检索与功能执行之间的差异，这为在成本高昂的部署评估之前的模型选择提供了信息。除了标准问答外，我们还使用确定性执行协议和新颖的交叉审查框架（CEF）评估操作能力，该框架在不依赖参考文本的情况下量化信息保真度和幻觉率。我们在异构任务套件中的评估揭示了关键的性能权衡：我们发现显著的知识执行差距，即静态检索的熟练程度并不能预测在临床计算或SQL生成等操作任务中的成功。此外，我们观察到被动安全（拒绝）与主动安全（错误检测）之间的差异，表明为高拒绝率而微调的模型往往无法可靠地审计临床文档的事实准确性。这些发现表明，没有单一架构在所有维度上占主导地位，强调了临床模型部署组合方法的必要性。我们还在 https://hf.co/spaces/m42-health/MEDIC-Benchmark 提供了公开的MEDIC排行榜。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the limitations of existing evaluation methods for Large Language Models (LLMs) in clinical applications, which often rely on static benchmarks that do not reflect real-world clinical workflows. Previous approaches have failed to adequately assess the operational capabilities of LLMs, leading to a disconnect between theoretical performance and practical utility. The proposed MEDIC framework introduces leading indicators of clinical competence across five dimensions, allowing for a more nuanced evaluation that identifies gaps in knowledge retrieval and functional execution. The methodology includes deterministic execution protocols and a novel Cross-Examination Framework (CEF) to measure information fidelity and hallucination rates without reference texts. The evaluation reveals critical performance trade-offs, such as a significant knowledge-execution gap and discrepancies between passive and active safety, underscoring the need for a diverse model deployment strategy. The findings support the development of a comprehensive MEDIC leaderboard to facilitate informed model selection.

GPT-Red: Automated Red Teaming via Self-Play at Scale
Authors: Eric Wallace, Christopher A. Choquette-Choo, Nikhil Kandpal, Sam Toyer, Dylan Hunn, Stephanie Lin, Yuxin Wen, Xiangyu Qi, Christopher Wolff, Zizhao Wang, Milad Nasr, Sicheng Zhu, Chuan Guo, Juan Felipe Cerón Uribe, Kaiwen Wang, Aiden Low, Kai Xiao, Kai Chen
First: 2026-07-28T16:03:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-28T16:03:39+00:00
Comments: 28 pages.13 main pages and 13 main figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
We introduce \textbf{GPT-Red}, an automated red-teaming agent that is trained to discover novel prompt injection attacks against frontier LLMs. The goal of this model is to evaluate and improve the robustness of our production systems. To this end, we use it to adversarially train GPT-5.6, our most robust model to prompt injections to date. To create GPT-Red, we design a scalable self-play algorithm where the model is tasked with attacking a diverse population of simultaneously-trained defender agents. We train the model on realistic red-teaming environments using compute on the same scale as some of our largest RL post-training runs, making it the single-largest LLM safety training run ever documented. GPT-Red excels at red-teaming: it reliably breaks our past models up to GPT-5.5, it finds more successful attacks than human red-teamers, and it generalizes to held-out environments, defender models, and harnesses. In the future, we expect that as we improve the robustness of each new GPT model, it will in turn will provide better learning signal for \textit{even stronger} red-teamer agents, thus unlocking a self-improvement flywheel.
中文标题/摘要
标题：GPT-Red：通过自我对弈实现自动化红队
我们介绍了\textbf{GPT-Red}，一个自动化红队代理，旨在发现针对前沿LLM的新型提示注入攻击。该模型的目标是评估和提高我们生产系统的鲁棒性。为此，我们利用它对GPT-5.6进行对抗性训练，这是迄今为止对提示注入最鲁棒的模型。为了创建GPT-Red，我们设计了一种可扩展的自我对弈算法，模型的任务是攻击一组同时训练的多样化防御代理。我们在现实的红队环境中训练该模型，使用的计算规模与我们一些最大的RL后训练运行相当，使其成为有史以来记录的最大LLM安全训练运行。GPT-Red在红队方面表现出色：它可靠地击破我们过去的模型（最高至GPT-5.5），找到的成功攻击次数超过人类红队员，并且能够推广到保留环境、防御模型和工具。在未来，我们预计随着我们提高每个新GPT模型的鲁棒性，它将为\textit{更强大的}红队代理提供更好的学习信号，从而解锁自我改进的飞轮。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the need for improved robustness in large language models (LLMs) against prompt injection attacks, which have been inadequately handled by previous methods that rely on human red-teaming efforts. The proposed approach, GPT-Red, introduces an automated red-teaming agent that utilizes a scalable self-play algorithm to generate and test novel attacks against a variety of defender models, significantly enhancing the efficiency and effectiveness of the red-teaming process. This paper contributes by documenting the largest LLM safety training run to date, demonstrating that GPT-Red can outperform human red-teamers and generalize across different environments and models. TRIDENT: Benchmarking LLM Safety in Finance, Medicine, and Law
Authors: Zheng Hui, Yijiang River Dong, Ehsan Shareghi, Nigel Collier
First: 2025-07-22T17:52:29+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-27T09:44:33+00:00
Comments: COLM 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
As large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-risk domains such as law, finance, and medicine, systematically evaluating their domain-specific safety and compliance becomes critical. While prior work has largely focused on improving LLM performance in these domains, it has often neglected the evaluation of domain-specific safety risks. To bridge this gap, we first define domain-specific safety principles for LLMs based on the AMA Principles of Medical Ethics, the ABA Model Rules of Professional Conduct, and the CFA Institute Code of Ethics. Building on this foundation, we introduce Trident-Bench, a benchmark specifically targeting LLM safety in the legal, financial, and medical domains. We evaluated 19 general-purpose and domain-specialized models on Trident-Bench and show that it effectively reveals key safety gaps -- strong generalist models (e.g., GPT, Gemini) can meet basic expectations, whereas domain-specialized models often struggle with subtle ethical nuances. This highlights an urgent need for finer-grained domain-specific safety improvements. By introducing Trident-Bench, our work provides one of the first systematic resources for studying LLM safety in law and finance, and lays the groundwork for future research aimed at reducing the safety risks of deploying LLMs in professionally regulated fields. Code and benchmark will be released at: https://github.com/zackhuiiiii/TRIDENT.
中文标题/摘要

ASEval: Automated Trajectory-Level Security Testing for Autonomous Agents
Authors: Jianan Ma, Xiaohu Du, Ruixiao Lin, Yaoxiang Bian, Jialuo Chen, Yunhao Feng, Xiaofang Yang, Shiwen Cui, Changhua Meng, Xinhao Deng, Jingyi Wang, Zhen Wang
First: 2026-05-21T11:07:51+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-24T06:09:29+00:00
Comments: 18 pages, 12 figures, 8 tables. Code and data available at https://github.com/antgroup/Agent3Sigma-Stage
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
As autonomous agents (e.g., OpenClaw) increasingly operate with deep system-level privileges to execute complex tasks, they introduce severe, unmitigated security risks. Existing LLM safety testing methods are largely built around prompt-level inputs and response-level judgments, while recent agent benchmarks remain limited in automation, trajectory coverage, and action-grounded judgment. In this work, we present ASEval, a novel automated security testing framework for autonomous agents. ASEval generates realistic benign multi-turn conversations, perturbs them into risk test cases through carefully designed perturbation operators, executes each case against the target agent, and applies an action-grounded oracle to judge the complete trace. We instantiate ASEval to construct A3S-Bench, a test suite of 2,254 executable multi-turn test cases spanning diverse usage scenarios, delivery patterns, and agent-level security risks. Extensive evaluation on 11 LLM-backed OpenClaw agents confirms that ASEval effectively exposes pervasive security failures. In particular, our proposed perturbation operators raise the average risk-trigger rate from 22.9% under existing injection techniques to 47.4%, as judged by an action-grounded oracle that matches human annotation at 93.2% binary F1. Our further analysis and case studies illustrate that such failures emerge from multi-turn delivery, persistent state, and tool-mediated actions that response-level evaluation overlooks.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ASEval：自动化轨迹级安全测试框架用于自主代理
随着自主代理（例如，OpenClaw）越来越多地以深层系统级权限执行复杂任务，它们引入了严重且未缓解的安全风险。现有的LLM安全测试方法主要围绕提示级输入和响应级判断构建，而最近的代理基准在自动化、轨迹覆盖和基于动作的判断方面仍然有限。在本研究中，我们提出了ASEval，一个新颖的自主代理自动化安全测试框架。ASEval生成现实的良性多轮对话，通过精心设计的扰动操作将其扰动为风险测试用例，针对目标代理执行每个用例，并应用基于动作的oracle来判断完整的轨迹。我们实例化ASEval构建了A3S-Bench，一个包含2,254个可执行多轮测试用例的测试套件，涵盖多种使用场景、交付模式和代理级安全风险。对11个基于LLM的OpenClaw代理的广泛评估确认ASEval有效暴露了普遍的安全失败。特别是，我们提出的扰动操作将现有注入技术下的平均风险触发率从22.9%提高到47.4%，根据与人类注释匹配的93.2%二元F1的基于动作的oracle进行判断。我们的进一步分析和案例研究表明，这些失败源于多轮交付、持续状态和工具介导的动作，而响应级评估则忽视了这些因素。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the significant security risks posed by autonomous agents operating with deep system-level privileges, highlighting the limitations of existing safety testing methods that focus primarily on prompt-level inputs and response-level evaluations. These traditional approaches lack automation, trajectory coverage, and action-grounded judgment, which ASEval aims to improve by introducing a novel automated security testing framework. ASEval generates realistic multi-turn conversations, transforms them into risk test cases using designed perturbation operators, and evaluates these cases against target agents with an action-grounded oracle. The framework contributes a test suite, A3S-Bench, consisting of 2,254 executable test cases that effectively expose security failures in 11 LLM-backed OpenClaw agents, achieving a risk-trigger rate increase from 22.9% to 47.4% and matching human annotation with a 93.2% binary F1 score, thus demonstrating its efficacy in addressing the identified gaps in existing methods.

Geometric Configurations of Perturbed Jailbreak Prompts
Authors: Lynn Delcon, Andres Algaba, Vincent Ginis
First: 2026-07-22T11:52:43+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-22T11:52:43+00:00
Comments: 21 pages, 9 figures, 7 tables, 2nd Workshop on Safe AI (SafeAI)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Perturbation techniques that turn unsuccessful jailbreak prompts into successful ones are continuously evolving, constituting a major security threat to LLM safety. In this paper, we investigate the internal representations of such string-level perturbed jailbreak inputs in the small weight models of the Qwen-2.5-1.5B/-3B/-7B-Instruct and Llama-3.2-1B/-3B/-3.1-8B-Instruct families. We select two representation spaces: the last-layer-last-token embedding space and the top-50 next-token probability space. The former space separates prompts based on their spelling and format, while the latter space is effectively one-dimensional but appears more complex to cluster. Within our refusal-dominated answer set we find no behavioral hyperplane in either space. Only the next token "Sure" in the 1.5B Qwen model, and both tokens "," and "ĊĊ" in the 1$ Llama model, display a significant association with a compliant-labeled answer.
中文标题/摘要
标题：扰动越狱提示的几何配置
扰动技术将不成功的越狱提示转变为成功的提示，持续演变，构成对大型语言模型安全的重大威胁。本文研究了Qwen-2.5-1.5B/-3B/-7B-Instruct和Llama-3.2-1B/-3B/-3.1-8B-Instruct系列的小权重模型中此类字符串级扰动越狱输入的内部表示。我们选择了两个表示空间：最后一层最后一个标记的嵌入空间和前50个下一个标记的概率空间。前者根据提示的拼写和格式进行分离，而后者有效地是一维的，但在聚类时显得更复杂。在我们的拒绝主导的答案集中，我们在任一空间中都未发现行为超平面。只有在1.5B Qwen模型中，下一个标记“Sure”，以及在1$ Llama模型中，两个标记“,”和“ĊĊ”与合规标记的答案显示出显著关联。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the evolving security threat posed by perturbation techniques that transform unsuccessful jailbreak prompts into successful ones, which is a significant concern for the safety of large language models (LLMs). Previous methods have primarily focused on surface-level analysis of these prompts, often failing to capture the underlying representations that contribute to their success or failure. This paper proposes a novel approach by investigating the internal representations of perturbed jailbreak inputs in specific embedding spaces of the Qwen and Llama model families, revealing that the last-layer-last-token embedding space and the top-50 next-token probability space provide distinct insights into prompt behavior. The findings indicate that within a refusal-dominated answer set, no behavioral hyperplane is present, with only specific tokens showing a significant association with compliant responses. This methodology contributes to a deeper understanding of how perturbations affect LLM behavior, achieving insights that support the goal of enhancing model safety.
本研究针对不断演变的扰动技术，这些技术将不成功的越狱提示转变为成功的提示，给大型语言模型（LLM）安全性带来了重大威胁。以往的方法主要集中在表面分析，往往忽视了这些提示的潜在表示，导致对其影响的理解不够全面。所提出的方法研究了Qwen和Llama模型系列中特定嵌入空间内的扰动越狱输入的内部表示，揭示了尽管最后一层最后一个令牌嵌入空间根据拼写和格式区分提示，但下一个令牌概率空间呈现出更复杂的聚类挑战。该论文的贡献在于展示了在拒绝主导的答案集中缺乏行为超平面，并识别出与合规响应相关的特定令牌，从而增强了对扰动下LLM行为的理解。该方法论涉及分析这些模型的内部表示，以评估其响应模式，获得的见解支持了提高LLM抵御扰动攻击的安全性目标。
Stress Testing Concept Erasure with Large Language Model Agents
Authors: Yuyang Xue, Feng Chen, Zhihua Liu, Edward Moroshko, Jingyu Sun, Steven McDonagh, Sotirios A. Tsaftaris
First: 2026-07-20T12:38:50+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-22T09:45:01+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Concept erasure aims to remove semantic concepts from a trained generative model and is increasingly important for responsible AI deployment. However, verifying whether a model has robustly removed targeted concepts remains a critical challenge. Existing evaluation methods are typically pre-defined and static, failing to expose vulnerabilities under diverse natural-language probes and challenging conditions. Moreover, manually designed evaluation strategies can be biased and difficult to scale. We posit that concept erasure evaluation is best formulated as an adaptive hypothesis search, operationalised by agents that iteratively propose, critique, and verify tests to systematically expand coverage of failure modes. To this end, we propose Stress Testing Agents for Concept Erasure (STACE), a framework that autonomously stress-tests concept-erased models using multiple Large Language Model (LLM) agents, by iteratively generating and verifying stress-testing hypotheses grounded by external knowledge. We also introduce a suite of metrics for assessing the performance and efficiency of LLM-agent-powered stress-testing frameworks. Our extensive experiments show that STACE outperforms five LLM-based evaluation baselines on four concept categories. Further analysis across two T2I models, six concept erasure approaches, and various erasure strengths show that STACE is robust for different settings. We also show that STACE can be adapted beyond concept erasure evaluation to other problem domains, such as LLM jailbreaking. Our code is available anonymously.
中文标题/摘要
标题：大型语言模型代理的概念消除压力测试
概念消除旨在从训练好的生成模型中移除语义概念，对于负责任的人工智能部署越来越重要。然而，验证模型是否稳健地移除了目标概念仍然是一个关键挑战。现有的评估方法通常是预定义和静态的，未能在多样的自然语言探测和挑战条件下暴露出脆弱性。此外，手动设计的评估策略可能存在偏见且难以扩展。我们认为，概念消除评估最好被表述为一种自适应假设搜索，由代理迭代提出、批评和验证测试，以系统性地扩展失败模式的覆盖范围。为此，我们提出了概念消除压力测试代理（STACE），这是一个框架，通过多个大型语言模型（LLM）代理自主地对概念消除模型进行压力测试，迭代生成和验证基于外部知识的压力测试假设。我们还引入了一套用于评估LLM代理驱动的压力测试框架的性能和效率的指标。我们的广泛实验表明，STACE在四个概念类别上优于五个基于LLM的评估基线。对两个T2I模型、六种概念消除方法和各种消除强度的进一步分析表明，STACE在不同设置下都表现稳健。我们还展示了STACE可以适应于概念消除评估以外的其他问题领域，如LLM越狱。我们的代码可匿名获取。

The proposed approach, Stress Testing Agents for Concept Erasure (STACE), differs from traditional methods by employing multiple Large Language Model agents to autonomously generate and verify hypotheses for stress-testing, thus expanding the coverage of potential failure modes. This method is well-motivated as it enhances the robustness of concept erasure evaluation. Don't Want Your LLM to Recommend Nuclear Strike? Try Asking It in Japanese
Authors: Rian Touchent
Venue: Proceedings of the 6th Workshop on Trustworthy NLP (TrustNLP 2026), Jul 2026, San Diego, United States. pp.489-502
First: 2026-07-21T10:09:45+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-21T10:09:45+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models are increasingly used in strategic and advisory contexts, yet their safety alignment is typically evaluated in English only. We test nine models from six providers and ask whether the language of a prompt can change a model's decision in a high-stakes scenario. We use single-turn game-theoretic vignettes in which a model advises a nuclear-armed nation on whether to strike a defenseless opponent. The prompt is intentionally amoral and strategically identical across languages. We find that Japanese prompts reduce launch rates in the Claude model family: Claude Sonnet 4.6 drops from 40% to 0% in scenarios where the strike is unnecessary and from 93% to 17% in contested scenarios, with minimal effect when the strike is strategically rational. The effect extends to Gemini Pro 3.1 (53% to 13%). A cross-language experiment isolates the mechanism: when instructed to reason in Japanese in an English prompt, launch rates drop from 93% to 37%. It is the language the model is asked to reason in, not the language of the input, that drives the effect. When reasoning in Japanese, models spontaneously generate moral vocabulary (''moral cost'', ''millions of lives'') that is entirely absent from the prompt. Five other models show no language effect, but they launch in nearly every condition regardless of language. The effect requires a model that already hesitates in English. These results show that LLM safety behavior is language-dependent, and that evaluating in English alone can miss both risks and safeguards encoded in other languages.
中文标题/摘要
标题：不想让你的大型语言模型推荐核打击？试试用日语问它
大型语言模型在战略和咨询环境中越来越多地被使用，但它们的安全对齐通常仅用英语进行评估。我们测试了来自六个提供商的九个模型，并询问提示的语言是否会在高风险场景中改变模型的决策。我们使用单回合博弈论小插曲，其中模型建议一个拥有核武器的国家是否对一个无防御的对手进行打击。提示在道德上是故意中立的，并在各语言中战略上相同。我们发现日语提示降低了Claude模型系列的发射率：在打击不必要的场景中，Claude Sonnet 4.6的发射率从40%降至0%，在争议场景中从93%降至17%，而在战略合理的情况下影响最小。该效应扩展到Gemini Pro 3.1（53%降至13%）。跨语言实验隔离了机制：当在英语提示中被指示用日语推理时，发射率从93%降至37%。影响是由模型被要求用何种语言推理驱动的，而不是输入的语言。当用日语推理时，模型自发生成道德词汇（“道德成本”，“数百万生命”），这些词汇在提示中完全缺失。其他五个模型没有语言效应，但在几乎所有条件下都发射，无论语言如何。该效应需要一个在英语中已经犹豫的模型。这些结果表明，LLM的安全行为依赖于语言，仅用英语评估可能会错过其他语言中编码的风险和保障。

LLM Safety Alignment in Low-Resource Languages: A Systematic Literature Review
Authors: Valdini Douglace Lemofouet, Blessing Ngozi Uzor, Paula Chikaodinaka Anyanwu, Danielle Blanche Kapsa, Sukairaj Hafiz Imam, P Sam Sahil, Abigail Oppong, Tassallah Abdullahi, Clemencia Siro, Idris Abdulmumin, Seid Muhie Yimam, Shamsuddeen Hassan Muhammad
Venue: IJCAI
First: 2026-07-20T21:11:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-20T21:11:00+00:00
Comments: The paper was accepted at LM4UC workshop organize by IJCAI. I added a screenshot of the decision (Open Review)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved substantial progress in safety alignment, yet their safety guarantees remain significantly weaker in low-resource and multilingual settings than in high-resource languages. In this paper, we conduct a Systematic Literature Review (SLR) of LLM safety alignment in low-resource languages by adopting the PRISMA 2020 methodology. Out of roughly 1,500 papers identified from Semantic Scholar, arXiv, and OpenAlex, 50 relevant studies have been selected and analyzed. Our review is organized around four themes: safety alignment methods, multilingual safety risks, evaluation benchmarks, and cross-lingual transferability. We further propose a taxonomy of safety alignment approaches based on three adaptation mechanisms: data adaptation, objective optimization, and mechanistic alignment. Across literature, translated English benchmarks fail to sufficiently represent culturally rooted harms, and multilingual models are more vulnerable to cross-lingual jailbreaks, code-switching attacks, and safety degradation in underrepresented languages. These failures are driven by several key factors, including uneven multilingual pre-training coverage, insufficient native-language preference data, poor transfer of safety representations, and a lack of culturally aware evaluation frameworks. The review also notes that many low-resource languages, especially African languages, have fewer safety benchmarks available than other multilingual regions. Overall, the results reveal a persistent multilingual safety gap, and suggest that future progress will require culturally grounded benchmarks, participatory data collection, balanced multilingual pre-training, and scalable multilingual alignment methods.

标题：低资源语言中的LLM安全对齐：系统文献综述
